These automated security tools scan your code repositories looking for exposed credentials like API keys, tokens or passwords. The âcfâ prefix makes Cloudflare tokens instantly recognizable with greater confidence, and the checksum makes it easy for tools to statically validate them. Your existing tokens will continue to work, but every new token you generate will use the scannable format so itâs easily detected with high confidence.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Credential Type&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;What it&amp;#39;s for&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;New Format&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;User API Key&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Legacy global API key tied to your user account (full access)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfk_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;User API Token&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Scoped token you create for specific permissions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfut_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Account API Token&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Token owned by the account (not a specific user)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfat_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;getting-started\&quot;&gt;Getting started&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#getting-started\&quot; Starting today, there is.Â Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Going forward, when a third party application requests access to your Cloudflare account, youâll be able to &lt;/tr&gt;\n&lt;/tbody&gt;&lt;/table&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;p&gt;Not all applications require the same permissions; some only need to read data, others may need to make changes to your Account. Understanding these scopes before you grant access helps you maintain least-privilege. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We also added a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dash.cloudflare.com/profile/access-management/authorization\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Connected Applications&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; experience so you can see which applications have access to which accounts, what scopes/permissions are associated with that application, and easily revoke that access as needed.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5Aiu82urkjaL9SWpZUBgNi/827cf38aa655d4094de1895d07f51137/BLOG-3216_5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3216 5\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1530\&quot; height=\&quot;527\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;getting-started\&quot;&gt;Getting started&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#getting-started\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The OAuth consent and revocation improvements are available now. Check which apps currently have access to your accounts by visiting My Profile &amp;gt; Access Management &amp;gt; Connected Applications.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For developers building integrations with Cloudflare, keep an eye on the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Changelog&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for more announcements around how you can register your own OAuth apps soon!Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;fine-grained-resource-level-permissioning\&quot;&gt;Fine-grained resource-level permissioningÂ &lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#fine-grained-resource-level-permissioning\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;If the token is the passport, then resource-scoped permissions are the visas inside it. Having a valid passport gets you through the front door, but it shouldn&amp;#39;t give you access to every room in the building. By narrowing the scope to specific resources â like a single Load Balancer pool or a specific Gateway policy â you are ensuring that even if an identity is verified, it only has the &amp;quot;visa&amp;quot; to go where itâs strictly necessary.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Last year, we &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog/post/2025-10-01-fine-grained-permissioning-beta/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;announced&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; support for resource scoped permissions in Cloudflareâs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/access-management/role-based-access-control-rbac/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;role-based access control (RBAC)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; system for several of our Zero Trust products. This enables you to right size permissions for both users and agents to minimize security risks. Weâve expanded this capability to several new resources-level permissions. The resource scope is now supported for:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Applications&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Identity Providers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Policies&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Service Tokens&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Targets&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâve also completely overhauled the API Token creation experience, making it easier for customers to provision and manage Account API Tokens right from the Cloudflare Dashboard. &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2XSjOtE46g8iVNN7QNzoDI/2f2280b106da9ebc9f3959d5300a4241/account_owned_token_gif.gif\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1280\&quot; height=\&quot;659\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;how-it-works\&quot;&gt;How it works&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-it-works\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When you add a member to your Cloudflare account or create an API Token, you typically assign that principal a policy. A Permission Policy is what gives a principal permission to take an action, whether thatâs managing Cloudflare One Access Applications, or DNS Records. Without a policy, a principal can authenticate, but they are unauthorized to do any actions within an account.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Policies are made up of three components: a Principal, a Role, and a Scope. The Principal is who or what you&amp;#39;re granting access to, whether that&amp;#39;s a human user, a Non-Human Identity (NHI) like an API Token, or increasingly, an Agent acting on behalf of a user. The Role defines what actions they&amp;#39;re permitted to take. The Scope determines where those permissions apply, and historically, that&amp;#39;s been restricted to the entire account, or individual zones.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;new-permission-roles\&quot;&gt;New permission roles&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#new-permission-roles\&quot; By refining how we manage permissions and credentials, developers and enterprises can have greater confidence in their security posture across the users, apps, agents, and scripts that access Cloudflare. Least privilege isnât a new concept, and for enterprises, itâs never been optional. Whether a human administrator is managing a zone or an agent is programmatically deploying a Worker, the expectation is the same, they should only be authorized to do the job it was given, and nothing else.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Following todayâs announcement, we recommend customers:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Review your &lt;a By adopting RFC 9728, agents can authenticate on behalf of users without using insecure service accounts.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;We have thousands of internal apps at Cloudflare. Some are things weâve built ourselves, others are self-hosted instances of software built by others. They range from business-critical apps nearly every person uses, to side projects and prototypes.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;All of these apps are protected by &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/policies/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Access&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. But when we started using and building agents âÂ particularly for uses beyond writing code â we hit a wall. People could access apps behind Access, but their agents couldnât.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access sits in front of internal apps. You define a policy, and then Access will send unauthenticated users to a login page to choose how to authenticate.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/3867rU5n4IkRilnfCAvOLD/8ec5ea3c25dbd6620a8644d231893732/BLOG-3146_2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3146 2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1633\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Example of a Cloudflare Access login page&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This flow worked great for humans. But all agents could see was a redirect to a login page that they couldnât act on.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Providing agents with access to internal app data is so vital that we immediately implemented a stopgap for our own internal use. We modified OpenCodeâs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://opencode.ai/docs/tools/#webfetch\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;web fetch tool&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; such that for specific domains, it triggered the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/tutorials/cli/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;cloudflared&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; CLI to open an authorization flow to fetch a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/access-management/token-based-authentication/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;JWT&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (JSON Web Token). By appending this token to requests, we enabled secure, immediate access to our internal ecosystem.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;While this solution was a temporary answer to our own dilemma, today weâre retiring this workaround and fixing this problem for everyone. Now in open beta, every Access application supports managed OAuth. One click to enable it for an Access app, and agents that speak OAuth 2.0 can easily discover how to authenticate (&lt;a href=\&quot;https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9728\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;RFC 9728&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;), send the user through the auth flow, and receive back an authorization token (the same JWT from our initial solution).Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Now, the flow works smoothly for both humans and agents. Cloudflare Access has a generous &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/plans/zero-trust-services/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;free tier&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. And building off our newly-introduced &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/organizations-beta/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Organizations beta&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, youâll soon be able to bridge identity providers across Cloudflare accounts too.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;how-managed-oauth-works\&quot;&gt;How managed OAuth works&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-managed-oauth-works\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;For a given internal app protected by Cloudflare Access, you enable managed OAuth in one click:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/79FSFNeaDbqn9DkSyfKrtz/5f0fd06ce9e127c06474263a529ec284/BLOG-3146_3.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3146 3\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1426\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Once managed OAuth is enabled, Cloudflare Access acts as the authorization server. It returns the &lt;code&gt;www-authenticate&lt;/code&gt; header, telling unauthorized agents where to look up information on how to get an authorization token. They find this at &lt;code&gt;https://&amp;lt;your-app-domain&amp;gt;/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server&lt;/code&gt;. Equipped with that direction, agents can just follow OAuth standards:Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;The agent dynamically registers itself as a client (a process known as Dynamic Client Registration âÂ &lt;a href=\&quot;https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7591\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;RFC 7591&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;),Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;The agent sends the human through a PKCE (Proof Key for Code Exchange) authorization flow (&lt;a href=\&quot;https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7636\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;RFC 7636&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;The human authorizes access, which grants a token to the agent that it can use to make authenticated requests on behalf of the user&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;Hereâs what the authorization flow looks like:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2LkJMuPCNX1Mu7MfEnFinH/fef5cf3f87e83d93c49ffeadbe6aed4d/BLOG-3146_4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3146 4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1406\&quot; height=\&quot;1999\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;If this authorization flow looks familiar, thatâs because itâs what the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Model Context Protocol (MCP)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; uses. We originally built support for this into our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/ai-controls/mcp-portals/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;MCP server portals&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; product, which proxies and controls access to many MCP servers, to allow the portal to act as the OAuth server. Now, weâre bringing this to all Access apps so agents can access not only MCP servers that require authorization, but also web pages, web apps, and REST APIs.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;mass-upgrading-your-internal-apps-to-be-agent-ready\&quot;&gt;Mass upgrading your internal apps to be agent-ready&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#mass-upgrading-your-internal-apps-to-be-agent-ready\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Upgrading the long tail of internal software to work with agents is a daunting task. In principle, in order to be agent-ready, every internal and external app would ideally have discoverable APIs, a CLI, a well-crafted MCP server, and have adopted the many emerging agent standards.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;AI adoption is not something that can wait for everything to be retrofitted. Most organizations have a significant backlog of apps built over many years. And many internal âappsâ work great when treated by agents as simple websites. For something like an internal wiki, all you really need is to enable &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/markdown-for-agents/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Markdown for Agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, turn on managed OAuth, and agents have what they need to read protected content.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To make the basics work across the widest set of internal applications, we use Managed OAuth. By putting Access in front of your legacy internal apps, you make them agent-ready instantly. No code changes, no retrofitting. Instead, just immediate compatibility.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;its-the-users-agent-no-service-accounts-and-tokens-needed\&quot;&gt;Itâs the userâs agent. No service accounts and tokens needed&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#its-the-users-agent-no-service-accounts-and-tokens-needed\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agents need to act on behalf of users inside organizations. One of the biggest anti-patterns weâve seen is people provisioning service accounts for their agents and MCP servers, authenticated using static credentials. These have their place in simple use cases and quick prototypes, and Cloudflare Access supports &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/service-credentials/service-tokens/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;service tokens&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for this purpose.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But the service account approach quickly shows its limits when fine-grained access controls and audit logs are required. We believe that every action an agent performs must be easily attributable to the human who initiated it, and that an agent must only be able to perform actions that its human operator is likewise authorized to do. Service accounts and static credentials become points at which attribution is lost. Agents that launder all of their actions through a service account are susceptible to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confused_deputy_problem\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;confused deputy problems&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and result in audit logs that appear to originate from the agent itself.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For security and accountability, agents must use security primitives capable of expressing this userâagent relationship. OAuth is the industry standard protocol for requesting and delegating access to third parties. It gives agents a way to talk to your APIs on behalf of the user, with a token scoped to the userâs identity, so that access controls correctly apply and audit logs correctly attribute actions to the end user.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;standards-for-the-win-how-agents-can-and-should-adopt-rfc-9728-in-their-web-fetch-tools\&quot;&gt;Standards for the win: how agents can and should adoptÂ RFC 9728 in their web fetch tools&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#standards-for-the-win-how-agents-can-and-should-adopt-rfc-9728-in-their-web-fetch-tools\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9728\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;RFC 9728&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is the OAuth standard that makes it possible for agents to discover where and how to authenticate. It standardizes where this information lives and how itâs structured. This RFC became official in April 2025 and was quickly adopted by the Model Context Protocol (MCP), which now &lt;a href=\&quot;https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/authorization\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;requires that both MCP servers and clients support it&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But outside of MCP, agents should adopt RFC 9728 for an even more essential use case: making requests to web pages that are protected behind OAuth and making requests to plain old REST APIs.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Most agents have a tool for making basic HTTP requests to web pages. This is commonly called the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/web-fetch-tool\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;âweb fetchâ tool&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Itâs similar to using the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Fetch_API\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;fetch()&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; API in JavaScript, often with some additional post-processing on the response. Itâs what lets you paste a URL into your agent and have your agent go look up the content.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, most agentsâ web fetch tools wonât do anything with the &lt;code&gt;www-authenticate&lt;/code&gt; header that a URL returns. The underlying model might choose to introspect the response headers and figure this out on its own, but the tool itself does not follow &lt;code&gt;www-authenticate&lt;/code&gt;, look up &lt;code&gt;/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server&lt;/code&gt;, and act as the client in the OAuth flow. But it &lt;i&gt;can&lt;/i&gt;, and we strongly believe it &lt;i&gt;should&lt;/i&gt;! Agents already do this to act as remote MCP clients.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To demonstrate this, weâve put up a draft pull request that &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/pull/22096/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;adapts the web fetch tool in Opencode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to show this in action. Before making a request, the adapted tool first checks whether it already has credentials ; if it does, it uses them to make the initial request. If the tool gets back a 401 or a 403 with a &lt;code&gt;www-authenticate&lt;/code&gt; header, it asks the user for consent to be sent through the serverâs OAuth flow.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Hereâs how that OAuth flow works. If you give the agent a URL that is protected by OAuth and complies with RFC 9728, the agent prompts the human for consent to open the authorization flow:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1vo4hGtVz7ovlNQslTvTal/3d2b1a861b9342842bd6297e1869aed4/BLOG-3146_5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3146 5\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1488\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;â¦sending the human to the login page:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/3867rU5n4IkRilnfCAvOLD/8ec5ea3c25dbd6620a8644d231893732/BLOG-3146_2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3146 2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1633\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;â¦and then to a consent dialog that prompts the human to grant access to the agent:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/39I0yjWXKGLNG4mhV5jEPQ/793559a5f18a04e807939b9560118ccb/BLOG-3146_6.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3146 6\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1818\&quot; height=\&quot;1644\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Once the human grants access to the agent, the agent uses the token it has received to make an authenticated request:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4e0mNkih5xtbAoQpwBrB32/701089e8ea256804890d45af08ef04ca/BLOG-3146_7.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3146 7\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1796\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Any agent from Codex to Claude Code to Goose and beyond can implement this, and thereâs nothing bespoke to Cloudflare. Itâs all built using OAuth standards.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We think this flow is powerful, and that supporting RFC 9728 can help agents with more than just making basic web fetch requests. If a REST API supports RFC 9728 (and the agent does too), the agent has everything it needs to start making authenticated requests against that API. If the REST API supports &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9727\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;RFC 9727&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, then the client can discover a catalog of REST API endpoints on its own, and do even more without additional documentation, agent skills, MCP servers or CLIs.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Each of these play important roles with agents â Cloudflare itself provides an &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode-mcp/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;MCP server for the Cloudflare API&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (built using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Code Mode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;), &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/commands/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Wrangler CLI&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/skills\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agent Skills&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://x.com/CloudflareDev/status/2037336582815175122\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Plugin&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. But supporting RFC 9728 helps ensure that even when none of these are preinstalled, agents have a clear path forward. If the agent has a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;sandbox to execute untrusted code&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;,Â it can just write and execute code that calls the API that the human has granted it access to. Weâre working on supporting this for Cloudflareâs own APIs, to help your agents understand how to use Cloudflare.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;coming-soon-share-one-identity-provider-idp-across-many-cloudflare-accounts\&quot;&gt;Coming soon: share one identity provider (IdP) across many Cloudflare accounts&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#coming-soon-share-one-identity-provider-idp-across-many-cloudflare-accounts\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;At Cloudflare our own internal apps are deployed to dozens of different Cloudflare accounts, which are all part of an Organization â a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/organizations-beta/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;newly introduced&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; way for administrators to manage users, configurations, and view analytics across many Cloudflare accounts. We have had the same challenge as many of our customers: each Cloudflare account has to separately configure an IdP, so Cloudflare Access uses our identity provider. Itâs critical that this is consistent across an organization â you donât want one Cloudflare account to inadvertently allow people to sign in just with a one-time PIN, rather than requiring that they authenticate via single-sign on (SSO).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To solve this, weâre currently working on making it possible to share an identity provider across Cloudflare accounts, giving organizations a way to designate a single primary IdP for use across every account in their organization.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;As new Cloudflare accounts are created within an organization, administrators will be able to configure a bridge to the primary IdP with a single click, so Access applications across accounts can be protected by one identity provider. This removes the need to manually configure IdPs account by account, which is a process that doesnât scale for organizations with many teams and individuals each operating their own accounts.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;whats-next\&quot;&gt;Whatâs next&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#whats-next\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Across companies, people in every role and business function are now using agents to build internal apps, and expect their agents to be able to access context from internal apps. We are responding to this step function growth in internal software development by making the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://workers.cloudflare.com/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers Platform&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; By integrating with Workers VPC, developers can now grant agents scoped access to private databases and APIs without manual tunnels.\n&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,true],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;AI agents have changed how teams think about private network access. Your coding agent needs to query a staging database. Your production agent needs to call an internal API. Your personal AI assistant needs to reach a service running on your home network. The clients are no longer just humans or services. They&amp;#39;re &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/what-is-agentic-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, running autonomously, making requests you didn&amp;#39;t explicitly approve, against infrastructure you need to keep secure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Each of these workflows has the same underlying problem: agents need to reach private resources, but the tools for doing that were built for humans, not autonomous software. VPNs require interactive login. SSH tunnels require manual setup. Exposing services publicly is a security risk. And none of these approaches give you visibility into what the agent is actually doing once it&amp;#39;s connected.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, &lt;b&gt;we&amp;#39;re introducing Cloudflare Mesh&lt;/b&gt; to connect your private networks together and provide secure access for your agents. We&amp;#39;re also integrating Mesh with &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Developer Platform&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; so that &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/products/workers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/products/durable-objects/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and agents built with the Agents SDK can reach your private infrastructure directly.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If youâre using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Oneâs SASE and Zero Trust suite&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, you already have access to Mesh. You donât need a new technology paradigm to secure agentic workloads. You need a SASE that was built for the agentic era, and thatâs Cloudflare One. Cloudflare Mesh is a new experience with a simpler setup that leverages the on-ramps youâre already familiar with: WARP Connector &lt;i&gt;(now called a Cloudflare Mesh node)&lt;/i&gt; and WARP Client &lt;i&gt;(now called Cloudflare One Client)&lt;/i&gt;. Together, these create a private network for human, developer, and agent traffic. Mesh is directly integrated into your existing Cloudflare One deployment. Your existing Gateway policies, Access rules, and device posture checks apply to Mesh traffic automatically.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you&amp;#39;re a developer who just wants private networking for your agents, services, and team, Mesh is where you start. Set it up in minutes, connect your networks, and secure your traffic. And because Mesh runs on the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare One&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; platform, you can grow into more advanced capabilities over time: &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/products/gateway/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Gateway&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; network, DNS, and HTTP policies for fine-grained traffic control, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/use-cases/infrastructure-access/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Access for Infrastructure&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for SSH and RDP session management, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/products/browser-isolation/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Browser Isolation&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for safe web access, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/data-loss-prevention/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;DLP&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to prevent sensitive data from leaving your network, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/products/casb/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;CASB&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for SaaS security. You wonât have to plan for all of this on day one. You just don&amp;#39;t have to migrate when you need it.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;new-agentic-workflows\&quot;&gt;New agentic workflows&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#new-agentic-workflows\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Private networking has always been about connecting clients to resources â SSH into a server, query a database, access an internal API. What&amp;#39;s changed is who the clients are. A year ago, the answer was your developers and your services. Today, it&amp;#39;s increasingly your agents.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This isn&amp;#39;t theoretical. Look at the ecosystem: the explosion of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/remote-model-context-protocol-servers-mcp/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;MCP (Model Context Protocol) servers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; providing tool access, coding agents that need to read from private repos and databases, personal assistants running on home hardware. Each of these patterns assumes the agent can reach the resources it needs. When those resources are isolated in private networks, the agent is stuck. &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/12RTZcOBkkKwmaRe5VYn9k/e1b00401bee274a7643a4dfd4139e2df/BLOG-3215_2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3215 2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1345\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;This creates three workflows that are hard to secure today:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Accessing a personal agent from a mobile device. You&amp;#39;re running OpenClaw on a Mac mini at home. You want to reach it from your phone, your laptop at a coffee shop, or your work machine. But exposing it to the public Internet (even behind a password) can leave some gaps exposed. Your agent has shell access, file system access, and network access to your home network. One misconfiguration and anyone can reach it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Letting a coding agent access your staging environment. You&amp;#39;re using Claude Code, Cursor, or Codex on your laptop. You ask it to check deployment status, query analytics from a staging database, or read from an internal object store. But those services live in a private cloud VPC, so your agent can&amp;#39;t reach them without exposing them to the Internet or tunneling your entire laptop into the VPC.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Connecting deployed agents to private services. You&amp;#39;re building agents into your product using the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents SDK&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; on Cloudflare Workers. Those agents need to call internal APIs, query databases, and access services that aren&amp;#39;t on the public Internet. They need private access, but with scoped permissions, audit trails, and no credential leakage.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;cloudflare-mesh-one-private-network-for-users-nodes-and-agents\&quot;&gt;Cloudflare Mesh: one private network for users, nodes, and agents&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#cloudflare-mesh-one-private-network-for-users-nodes-and-agents\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cloudflare Mesh is developer-friendly private networking. One lightweight connector, one binary, connects everything: your personal devices, your remote servers, your user endpoints. You don&amp;#39;t need to install separate tools for each pattern. One connector on your network, and every access pattern works.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Once connected, devices in your private network can talk to each other over private IPs, routed through Cloudflareâs global network across 330+ cities giving you better reliability and control over your network.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/ENfLb0kr1Zesh7PnuCvDK/830afd93d49b658023a9ece55e0e505b/BLOG-3215_3.gif\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3215 3\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1800\&quot; height=\&quot;761\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Now, with Mesh, a single solution can solve all of the agent scenarios we mentioned above:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;With &lt;b&gt;Cloudflare One Client for iOS&lt;/b&gt; on your phone, you can securely connect your mobile devices to your local Mac mini running OpenClaw via a Mesh private network.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;With &lt;b&gt;Cloudflare One Client for macOS&lt;/b&gt; on your laptop, you can connect your laptop to your private network so your coding agents can reach staging databases or APIs and query them.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;With &lt;b&gt;Mesh nodes&lt;/b&gt; on your Linux servers, you can connect VPCs in external clouds together, letting agents access resources and MCPs in external private networks.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Because Mesh is powered by &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/team-and-resources/devices/cloudflare-one-client/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare One Client&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, every connection inherits the security controls of the Cloudflare One platform. Gateway policies apply to Mesh traffic. Device posture checks validate connecting devices. DNS filtering catches suspicious lookups. You get this without additional configuration: the same policies that protect your human traffic protect your agent traffic.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;choosing-between-mesh-and-tunnel\&quot;&gt;Choosing between Mesh and Tunnel&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#choosing-between-mesh-and-tunnel\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;With the introduction of Mesh, you might ask: when should I use Mesh instead of Tunnel? Both connect external networks privately to Cloudflare, but they serve different purposes. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/networks/connectors/cloudflare-tunnel/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Tunnel&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is the ideal solution for unidirectional traffic, where Cloudflare proxies the traffic from the edge to specific private services (like a web server or a database).Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare Mesh, on the other hand, provides a full bidirectional, many-to-many network. Every device and node on your Mesh can access one another using their private IPs. An application or agent running in your network can discover and access any other resource on the Mesh without each resource needing its own Tunnel.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;using-the-power-of-cloudflares-network\&quot;&gt;Using the power of Cloudflareâs network&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#using-the-power-of-cloudflares-network\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cloudflare Mesh gives you the benefits of a mesh network (resiliency, high scalability, low latency and high performance), but, by routing everything through Cloudflare, it resolves a key challenge of mesh networks: NAT traversal.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Most of the Internet is behind NAT (Network Address Translation). This mechanism allows an entire local network of devices to share a single public IP address by mapping traffic between public headers and private internal addresses. When two devices are behind NAT, direct connections can fail and traffic has to fall back to relay servers. If your relay infrastructure has limited points of presence, a meaningful fraction of your traffic hits those relays, adding latency and reducing reliability. And while it can be possible to self-host your own relay servers to compensate, that means taking on the burden of managing additional infrastructure just to connect your existing network.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare Mesh takes a different approach. All Mesh traffic routes through Cloudflare&amp;#39;s global network, the same infrastructure that serves traffic for some of the largest websites of the Internet. For cross-region or multi-cloud traffic, this consistently beats public Internet routing. There&amp;#39;s no degraded fallback path, because the Cloudflare edge is the path.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Routing through Cloudflare also means every packet passes through Cloudflare&amp;#39;s security stack. This is the key advantage of building Mesh on the Cloudflare One platform: security isn&amp;#39;t a separate product you bolt on later. And by leveraging this same global backbone, we can provide these core pillars to every team from day one:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;50 nodes and 50 users free. &lt;/b&gt;Your whole team and your whole staging environment on one private network, included with every Cloudflare account.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Global edge routing.&lt;/b&gt; 330+ cities, optimized backbone routing. No relay servers with limited points of presence. No degraded fallback paths.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Security controls from day one.&lt;/b&gt; Mesh runs on Cloudflare One. Gateway policies, DNS filtering, DLP, traffic inspection, and device posture checks are all available on the same platform. Start with simple private connectivity. Turn on Gateway policies when you need traffic filtering. Enable Access for Infrastructure when you need session-level controls for SSH and RDP. Add DLP when you need to prevent sensitive data from leaving your network. Every capability is one toggle away.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;High availability.&lt;/b&gt; Create a Mesh node with high availability enabled and spin up multiple connectors using the same token in active-passive mode. They advertise the same IP routes, so if one goes down, traffic fails over automatically.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;integrated-with-the-developer-platform-with-workers-vpc\&quot;&gt;Integrated with the Developer Platform with Workers VPC&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#integrated-with-the-developer-platform-with-workers-vpc\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Mesh connects your agents and resources across external clouds, but you also need to be able to connect from your agents built on Workers with Agents SDK as well. To enable this, weâve extended &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/workers-vpc-open-beta/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers VPC&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to make your entire Mesh network accessible to Workers and Durable Objects.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;That means that you can connect to your Cloudflare Mesh network from Workers, making the entire network accessible from a single bindingâs &lt;code&gt;fetch()&lt;/code&gt; call. This complements Workers VPCâs existing support for Cloudflare Tunnel, giving you more choice over how you want to secure your networks. Now, you can specify entire networks that you want to connect to in your &lt;code&gt;wrangler.jsonc&lt;/code&gt; file. To bind to your Mesh network, use the &lt;code&gt;cf1:network&lt;/code&gt; reserved keyword that binds to the Mesh network of your account:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Shell\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Shell\&quot;&gt;&amp;quot;vpc_networks&amp;quot;: [\n  { &amp;quot;binding&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;MESH&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;network_id&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;cf1:network&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;remote&amp;quot;: true },\n  { &amp;quot;binding&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;AWS_VPC&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;tunnel_id&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;350fd307-...&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;remote&amp;quot;: true }\n]\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Then, you can use it within your Worker or agent code:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;export default {\n  async fetch(request: Request, env: Env, ctx: ExecutionContext) {\n    // Reach any internal host on your Mesh, no pre-registration required\n    const apiResponse = await env.MESH.fetch(&amp;quot;http://10.0.1.50/api/data&amp;quot;);\n\n    // Internal hostname resolved via tunnel&amp;#039;s private DNS resolver\n    const dbResponse = await env.AWS_VPC.fetch(&amp;quot;http://internal-db.corp.local:5432&amp;quot;);\n\n    return new Response(await apiResponse.text());\n  },\n};\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;By connecting the Developer Platform to your Mesh networks, you can build Workers that have secure access to your private databases, internal APIs and MCPs, allowing you to build cross-cloud agents and MCPs that provide agentic capabilities to your app. But it also opens up a world where agents can autonomously observe your entire stack end-to-end, cross-reference logs and suggest optimizations in real-time.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;how-it-all-fits-together\&quot;&gt;How it all fits together&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-it-all-fits-together\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Together, Cloudflare Mesh, Workers VPC, and the Agents SDK provide a unified private network for your agents that spans both Cloudflare and your external clouds. Weâve merged connectivity and compute so your agents can securely reach the resources they need, wherever they live, across the globe.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6tYFghWEN1H3jIwV0Kejc9/04dcaedda5692a9726a7081d19bc413c/BLOG-3215_4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3215 4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1345\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Mesh nodes&lt;/b&gt; are your servers, VMs, and containers. They run a headless version of Cloudflare One Client and get a Mesh IP. Services talk to services over private IPs, bidirectionally, routed through Cloudflare&amp;#39;s edge.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Devices&lt;/b&gt; are your laptops and phones. They run the Cloudflare One Client and reach Mesh nodes directly: SSH, database queries, API calls, all over private IPs. Your local coding agents use this connection to access private resources.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Agents on Workers&lt;/b&gt; reach private services through Workers VPC Network bindings. They get scoped access to entire networks, mediated by MCP. The network enforces what the agent can reach. The MCP server enforces what the agent can do.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;whats-next\&quot;&gt;Whatâs next&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#whats-next\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The current version of Mesh provides the foundation for secure, unified connectivity. But as agentic workflows become more complex, weâre focused on moving beyond simple connectivity toward a network that is more intuitive to manage and more granularly aware of who, or what, is talking to your services. Here is what we are building for the rest of the year.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;hostname-routing\&quot;&gt;Hostname routing&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#hostname-routing\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;We&amp;#39;re extending Cloudflare Tunnel&amp;#39;s &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/tunnel-hostname-routing/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;hostname routing&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to Mesh this summer. Your Mesh nodes will be able to attract traffic for private hostnames like &lt;code&gt;wiki.local&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;api.staging.internal&lt;/code&gt;, without you having to manage IP lists or worry about how those hostnames resolve on the Cloudflare edge. Route traffic to services by name, not by IP. If your infrastructure uses dynamic IPs, auto-scaling groups, or ephemeral containers, this removes an entire class of routing headaches.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;mesh-dns\&quot;&gt;Mesh DNS&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#mesh-dns\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Today, you reach Mesh nodes by their Mesh IPs: &lt;code&gt;ssh 100.64.0.5&lt;/code&gt;. That works, but it&amp;#39;s not how you think about your infrastructure. You think in names: &lt;code&gt;postgres-staging&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;api-prod&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;nikitas-openclaw&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Later this year we&amp;#39;re building Mesh DNS so that every node and device that joins your Mesh automatically gets a routable internal hostname. No DNS configuration or manual records. Add a node named &lt;code&gt;postgres-staging&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;postgres-staging.mesh&lt;/code&gt; resolves to the right Mesh IP from any device on your Mesh.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Combined with hostname routing, you&amp;#39;ll be able to &lt;code&gt;ssh postgres-staging.mesh&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;curl http://api-prod.mesh:3000/health&lt;/code&gt; without ever knowing or managing an IP address.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;identity-aware-routing\&quot;&gt;Identity-aware routing&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#identity-aware-routing\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Today, Mesh nodes authenticate to the Cloudflare edge, but they share an identity at the network layer. Devices authenticate with user identity via the Cloudflare One Client, but nodes don&amp;#39;t yet carry distinct, routable identities that Gateway policies can differentiate.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We want to change that. The goal is identity-aware routing for Mesh, where each node, each device, and eventually each agent gets a distinct identity that policies can evaluate. Instead of writing rules based on IP ranges, you write rules based on who or what is connecting.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This matters most for agents. Today, when an agent running on Workers calls a tool through a VPC binding, the target service sees a Worker making a request. It doesn&amp;#39;t know which agent is calling, who authorized it, or what scope was granted. On the Mesh side, when a local coding agent on your laptop reaches a staging service, Gateway sees your device identity but not the agent&amp;#39;s.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We&amp;#39;re working toward a model where agents carry their own identity through the network:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Principal / Sponsor: The human who authorized the action (Nikita from the platform team)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Agent: The AI system performing it (the deployment assistant, session #abc123)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Scope: What the agent is allowed to do (read deployments, trigger rollbacks, nothing else)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;This would let you write policies like: reads from Nikita&amp;#39;s agents are allowed, but writes require Nikita directly. Agent traffic can be filtered independently from human traffic. An agent&amp;#39;s network access can be revoked without touching Nikita&amp;#39;s.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The infrastructure for this is in place. Mesh nodes provision with per-node tokens, devices authenticate with per-user identity, and Workers VPC bindings scope per-service access. The missing piece is making these identities visible to the policy layer so Gateway can make routing and access decisions based on them. That&amp;#39;s what we&amp;#39;re building.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;mesh-in-containers\&quot;&gt;Mesh in containers&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#mesh-in-containers\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Today, Mesh nodes run on VMs and bare-metal Linux servers. But modern infrastructure increasingly runs in containers: Kubernetes pods, Docker Compose stacks, ephemeral CI/CD runners. We&amp;#39;re building a Mesh Docker image that lets you add a Mesh node to any containerized environment.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This means you&amp;#39;ll be able to include a Mesh sidecar in your Docker Compose stack and give every service in that stack private network access. A microservice running in a container in your staging cluster could reach a database in your production VPC over Mesh, without either service needing a public endpoint. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;It is also useful for CI/CD pipelines that can access private infrastructure during builds and tests: your GitHub Actions runner pulls the Mesh container image, joins your network, runs integration tests against your staging environment, and tears down. All without VPN credentials to manage or persistent tunnels to maintain: the node disappears when the container exits.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We expect the Mesh Docker image to be available later this year.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;get-started\&quot;&gt;Get started&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#get-started\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;While we continue to evolve these identity and routing capabilities, the foundation for secure, unified networking is available today. You can start bridging your clouds and securing your agents in just a few minutes.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Get started Cloudflare Mesh&lt;/b&gt;: Head to Networking &amp;gt; Mesh in the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/mesh\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare dashboard&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Free for up to 50 nodes and 50 users.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Build agents with Agents SDK and Workers VPC: &lt;/b&gt;Install the Agents SDK (`npm i agents`), follow the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-vpc/get-started/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers VPC quickstart&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and build a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/guides/remote-mcp-server/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;remote MCP server&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; with private backend access.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Already on Cloudflare One?&lt;/b&gt; Mesh works with your existing setup. Your Gateway policies, device posture checks, and access rules apply to Mesh traffic automatically. See &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/networks/connectors/cloudflare-mesh/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;the Mesh documentation&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to add your first node.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image For example, we now make Cloudflareâs entire API available in a single Code Mode MCP server that uses &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode-mcp/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;less than 1,000 tokens&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Thereâs a lot more surface area to cover, though: &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/commands/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;CLI commands&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/workers-environment-live-object-bindings/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers Bindings&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; âÂ including APIs for local development and testing. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/api/reference/sdks/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;SDKs&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; across multiple languages.
